Flood Loss & Trigger Lab visual tests, generated from records
crc-lab

Visual tests and source facts

One card per check record: what ran, the rules and whether each passed. Thresholds marked proposed are starting values. Parameters carry their tags [S] [I] [P] [V] (docs/flood/requirements.md, section 4). Sources list the outcome of each fact read from the primary source (FS6), with the level of check named as in FS7.

Outlines closer than 150 m are one site: 108 outlines that are not rail make 90 sites, of which 28 have an outline within 100 m of flooding on the city's 2.13 in/hr map. Windows passing the rule: 6 counting sites, 15 counting outlines that are not rail, 25 counting every outline.

Software: crc-framework 1.0.0, crc-sdk 1.0.0, python 3.12.11

SFINCS and HydroMT-SFINCS stood up; toy model timed at three sizes

F0 pass

Record f0-solver-timing, written 2026-11-25T19:08:07Z.

  • Wall time is measured around 'docker run' and includes container start.
  • Parallel batches: the runs start together; batch wall time is the longest run.
  • Every toy value is a placeholder [P]; the toy exists to time the solver.
  • Catalogue size: plan F0's rule sets 150 runs, since one run at 600,000 cells is estimated at 425 s, under 600 s. Those 150 runs take about 17.7 hours of solver time one after another on this machine, more than the 12 hours used as a yardstick (about 101 runs fit in 12 hours). Running in parallel does not shorten it: 2 runs at once gave 0.96 times the throughput of runs one after another; 4 runs at once gave 0.89 times the throughput of runs one after another. Real terrain with buildings may need a smaller time step than the toy, which would make each run longer.

Terrain: 1 m lidar, 5 m grid, water, deep patches and a check on datum and units

F1 pass

Record f1-terrain, written 2026-11-25T21:01:27Z.

  • hillshade.png: shading exaggerates nothing but hides absolute height; water is the F1 rule, not a mapped shoreline; the study area outline comes from census-based neighbourhood outlines simplified to 1 m.
  • elevation.png: the colour scale is fixed at -2 to 30 m, so the river (flat -1.62 m) and the deep patches (below -5 m) share the bottom colour; the black line is the outline of the land in the city's neighbourhood areas, not a surveyed shoreline.
  • footprint_difference.png: 17728 footprints; the city's ground elevation is a lowest-adjacent-grade value in feet whose accuracy varies by source and is not independent of lidar for every footprint; the terrain side is the median of 1 m pixels 1 to 3 m outside the footprint, leaving out water and other footprints; values beyond +-3 m are drawn in the end bins (28 of them). It tests units and vertical datum; a horizontal offset of about 1 m would barely show in it.
  • deep_patches.png: patches are 8-connected pixels below -5 m; what they are is read from shape and position, not from a map of the place (see the notes on the patches).
  • subgrid_range.png: maximum minus minimum of the 25 pixels of 1 m in each 5 m cell, on bare earth; walls and buildings are not in the terrain, so the relief a cell hides in the city is larger than shown. Scale fixed at 0 to 5 m.
  • Horizontal datum: PROJ 9.8.1 (network off) uses 'Inverse of UTM zone 18N + NAD83 to WGS 84 (1) + UTM zone 18N' between EPSG:26918 and EPSG:32618, a null operation (largest shift at the corners and centre 1.15e-04 m); the more accurate operations need grids that are not installed. The terrain is therefore cut on its own pixel grid and relabelled, not resampled. Its position carries the NAD83-to-WGS84 difference: 1.1 m here (-0.403 m east, 1.024 m north from NAD83 to WGS 84 (G1762) at epoch 2013.948, by 'Conversion from NAD83(2011) (geog2D) to NAD83(2011) (geocentric) + Inverse of IT...', accuracy 0.01 m), so a point labelled (x, y) on the terrain lies about that far from the WGS84 position of the same name. The tile's NAD83 is assumed to be NAD83(2011).
  • Deep patch 1: 15274 m², minimum -13.49 m, median -12.84 m, at 40.70253 N 73.97067 W, near 'Brooklyn Navy Yard' (Brooklyn); 1% of its pixels inside the land outlines, 1% in the study area; fits a rectangle 330.0 m by 50.1 m filled to 92%; 83% of its pixels lie within 0.5 m of its 10th percentile (a flat floor); 0% of the pixels touching it are flattened water; their median is -0.42 m.
  • Deep patch 2: 15633 m², minimum -13.31 m, median -12.84 m, at 40.70150 N 73.97101 W, near 'Brooklyn Navy Yard' (Brooklyn); 8% of its pixels inside the land outlines, 8% in the study area; fits a rectangle 339.3 m by 49.5 m filled to 93%; 86% of its pixels lie within 0.5 m of its 10th percentile (a flat floor); 0% of the pixels touching it are flattened water; their median is -0.29 m.
  • Deep patch 3: 1939 m², minimum -8.14 m, median -7.33 m, at 40.70140 N 73.97601 W, near 'Brooklyn Navy Yard' (Brooklyn); 100% of its pixels inside the land outlines, 100% in the study area; fits a rectangle 107.4 m by 21.6 m filled to 84%; 44% of its pixels lie within 0.5 m of its 10th percentile (a flat floor); 0% of the pixels touching it are flattened water; their median is -4.27 m.
  • Deep patches, reading. Evidence: every patch lies in or beside the outline named Brooklyn Navy Yard, is a near-rectangle (fill of its rectangle above), mostly with a flat floor, and no flattened-water pixel touches it, so a rim of terrain closes it off from the flattened river. The two large ones lie in slips that the city's outlines leave out of the land. Guess, not checked against a map or record of the yard: they are the yard's dry docks, drained when the lidar was flown; the two of about 330 m by 50 m look like the two largest docks, the small one on land like an older, smaller dock. For the flood model they are pits 7 to 13 m deep: the parts inside the study area (see share_in_study_area) would hold water that in reality may be held out by a gate, or be already filled; F3 decides how to treat them.
  • Sub-grid tables (HydroMT-SFINCS 2.0.0): 640000 active cells of 640000, 215 MB, 45.4 s with 40.1 s for the tables, peak memory 0.98 GiB. Grid only: no forcing, no boundary, no run; roughness uniform [P]. The tables' cell minimum is not the minimum of the 25 pixels of 1 m: 54% of cells agree within 0.01 m and 98.2% within 0.1 m (percentiles 0, 1, 50, 99, 100 of the difference: -4.68, -0.14, -0.00, +0.04, +1.14 m); HydroMT samples the terrain at 0.5 m, and how it interpolates was not read from its code.

DELUGE: Towards Continental-Scale Daily Pluvial Flood Damage Prediction via Interpretable Conditioning on Foundation Model Embeddings (arXiv 2607.16050), section 3.1.1 arXiv (Cornell University); authors Kawakami, Cayan, Liu, Ma, Corringham; facts checked: 8 confirmed, 1 not checked; 8 confirmed, 0 differs, 1 not checked, 3 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FT5. Known biases: The rule was fitted to NFIP claims 2017-2022 in the 100 highest-claim 75 km cells of the conterminous US (about 81% of pluvial claims), which are in the Northeast and Gulf regions; the shares 81.1/12.9/6.0 describe those claims, not New York City alone. AORC rainfall at the claim location is the only judge of a correct date, so claims for non-rain causes (tidal, river) are also dropped or moved.

Facts of DELUGE: Towards Continental-Scale Daily Pluvial Flood Damage Prediction via Interpretable Conditioning on Foundation Model Embeddings (arXiv 2607.16050), section 3.1.1. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

rule_text furtherconfirmed10 mm threshold on daily maximum hourly AORC rainfall, window d-3 to d+3Confirmed as stated in requirements FT5: step 1 daily maximum of hourly accumulated precipitation from NOAA AORC at the claim location for day d; step 2 keep d if it exceeds 10 mm; step 3 otherwise take the day in d-3..d+3 with the largest such value and move the claim to it if that exceeds 10 mm; step 4 otherwise discard the claim.https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.16050
passage
"3.1.1 Temporal Uncertainty Correction. The dateOfLoss field in the NFIP claims data is known to be noisy where reported dates of loss often fail to align with precipitation events in the historical precipitation records. ... To mitigate this noise, we apply the following correction to the dateOfLoss field d: (1) Compute the daily maximum hourly accumulated precipitation from NOAA AORC [21] for day d at the location of the claim. (2) If the daily maximum hourly accumulated precipitation on day d exceeds 10 mm, accept dateOfLoss as reported. (3) Otherwise, search the window d' in {d - 3, ..., d + 3} and select the day with the largest daily maximum hourly precipitation. If this maximum exceeds 10 mm, update dateOfLoss to d'. (4) If no day's daily maximum hourly precipitation in the window exceeds 10 mm, discard the claim as erroneous."
Threshold rationale in the paper: "We adopt 10 mm as a conservative threshold for maximum hourly accumulated precipitation, sitting between two reference points" (FFG about 25.4 mm; AMS heavy precipitation 7.62 mm in one hour). The paper does not say what rainfall location uncertainty (0.1 degree claim position) does to the daily maximum; the rule is applied "at the location of the claim" after the spatial correction of section 3.1.2.
shares furtherconfirmed81.1%, 12.9%, 6.0%81.1% accepted as reported, 12.9% shifted within +/-3 days, 6.0% discarded. Appendix Table 6 gives the shift distribution in percent: -3: 2.0, -2: 1.9, -1: 5.0, 0: 81.1, +1: 2.0, +2: 1.4, +3: 0.6, discarded: 6.0 (sums to 100.0; the six non-zero shifts sum to 12.9).https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.16050
passage
"With this processing, 81.1% of claims are accepted as reported, 12.9% are shifted within ±3 days, and 6.0% are discarded." ; Table 6: Δ (days) -3 -2 -1 0 +1 +2 +3 – ; % 2.0 1.9 5.0 81.1 2.0 1.4 0.6 6.0
The appendix prose says "7% has shifts of ±1 day" (5.0 + 2.0), consistent with the table. Claims counted are NFIP claims with causeOfDamage = rainfall or snowmelt in the study cells (the paper filters to that cause), not all claims.
data_version_used furthernot applicable–The paper uses "OpenFEMA FIMA NFIP Redacted Claims Dataset v2"; the current OpenFEMA version is v3 (see source nfip-redacted-claims).https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.16050
passage
"Our prediction target is derived from the OpenFEMA FIMA NFIP Redacted Claims Dataset v2 [24], which records every NFIP insurance claim ... including the reported dateOfLoss, redacted location (Census Tract, ZIP code, and nearest 0.1° × 0.1° grid cell), causeOfDamage category" ; claims filtered to "causeOfDamage = Accumulation of rainfall or snowmelt"
Shares 81.1/12.9/6.0 cannot be expected to repeat on v3 claims in New York; FT5 asks to report the same three shares for the lab data, which is a comparison, not a replication. No claim in the requirements to compare; kept as a note for F8.

discount_rate furtherconfirmed7% since 2025-04-08, from 'News and Notes About Benefit-Cost Analysis' posted 2025-06-17Confirmed: on 8 April (2025) OMB revoked the November 2023 update of Circular A-94 (3.1%) and reinstated the 1992 version, returning the rate to 7%; HMA projects not obligated/awarded by FEMA as of 8 April 2025 must use 7%; Public Assistance projects not awarded by then should use 7%. Page 'Last updated June 17, 2025'. The snapshot text gives the day and month but not the year; the 2025 year follows from the 'April 8, 2025' in the HMA sentence.http://web.archive.org/web/20250914063508/https://www.fema.gov/grants/guidance-tools/benefit-cost-analysis/news
passage
On April 8, the Office of Management and Budget (OMB) revoked the November 2023 update to OMB Circular No. A-94 ..., which had set the discount rate at 3.1%. OMB reinstated its October 1992 version of Circular A-94, returning the discount rate to 7%. ... Hazard Mitigation Assistance projects that have not been obligated/awarded by FEMA as of April 8, 2025, must use the 7% discount rate when using a Benefit-Cost Analysis (BCA) to calculate cost effectiveness. ... Last updated June 17, 2025

FloodNet: Street Flooding Events Measured by FloodNet Sensors NYC Department of Environmental Protection (DEP), data from FloodNet (NYU and CUNY), on NYC Open Data; facts confirmed; values to be recomputed by a second reader in F2; 12 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 1 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FS3, FV2, FT1. Known biases: Sensors sit only where the project could mount them (street furniture, mostly over sidewalks), usually above the local low point, so shallow floods and floods in the roadbed below curb height are missed; a sensor offline, not yet installed or retired records nothing, and the tables do not say when a sensor was offline. The network grew from 9 sensors in September 2021 to 68 in September 2023, so a count of sensors with events is mostly a count of where sensors were.

Facts of FloodNet: Street Flooding Events Measured by FloodNet Sensors. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

{"code":"feature_deprecated","deprecatedAt":1791331200,"deprecationInfoLink":"https://support.socrata.com/hc/en-us/articles/43491231777047","error":true,"message":"This feature has been deprecated","sunsetAt":1797897600}
Outcome is about the download address, not the dataset id. The deprecation notice page was behind a bot check and could not be read; whether /resource/<id>.csv is also affected is not known. Both tables were fetched in full with /resource/<id>.csv?$limit=50000; the 3611 and 496 rows equal the count(*) from the API, so nothing was cut. The identifier is as the requirements give it. The retired bulk-download address was not a claim of the requirements; the reader uses the resource address.
fieldsconfirmed–13 fields. sensor_name (text), sensor_id (text; key to the sensor table), flood_start_time and flood_end_time (timestamps, GMT), max_depth_inches (inches), onset_time_mins, drain_time_mins, duration_mins, duration_above_4_inches_mins, duration_above_12_inches_mins, duration_above_24_inches_mins (minutes; drain_time_mins empty in 1 row), flood_profile_depth_inches (JSON array, inches) and flood_profile_time_secs (JSON array, seconds from flood start). A flood event is a series of depth measurements above 10 mm, one-minute sampling, reviewed by a neural-network classifier and by a person. No borough or coordinates in this table.https://data.cityofnewyork.us/api/views/aq7i-eu5q.json
passage
max_depth_inches | number | The maximum flood depth (in inches) measured at the sensor location during the event
flood_start_time | calendar_date | Start time of the measured flood event (i.e., time when flood waters began accumulating beneath sensor, in GMT)
duration_above_4_inches_mins | number | The amount of time during the flood event that measured water depths were equal to or greater than 4 inches.
A flood event is defined as a series of water depth measurements greater than 10 mm; all measured flood events presented here have undergone QC by a member of the FloodNet team.
Depth is as measured under the sensor, not at the lowest point of the street: see sensor table field lowest_point_height_delta_inches. 2 events (Q-davenport-ct-2-07znk0, 2021-01-16 and 2021-02-02) start before the sensor date_installed (2021-03-05).

Matching rule: an event belongs to a calendar date when its flood_start_time (stated to be GMT) converted to America/New_York local time falls on that date; the borough of the event is the borough of its sensor_id in kb2e-tjy3 (the events table has no borough). Alternative rules are given in the note. Counts are of distinct sensor_id. If the GMT date is used instead, 2021-09-01 has 0 events and 2021-09-02 has the same 5 sensors (Brooklyn 3, Queens 2): the whole Ida rainfall of the evening of 1 September local time falls on 2 September GMT. Counting events whose [start, end] interval overlaps the local day: 2021-09-01 gives the same 5; 2021-09-02 local gives 3 sensors (Brooklyn 1, Queens 2) because three events run past local midnight.

FloodNet: Sensor Deployment Metadata NYC Department of Environmental Protection (DEP), data from FloodNet (NYU and CUNY), on NYC Open Data; facts confirmed; values to be recomputed by a second reader in F2; 9 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 1 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FS3, FV2, FT1. Known biases: Sensors sit only where the project could mount them (street furniture, mostly over sidewalks), usually above the local low point, so shallow floods and floods in the roadbed below curb height are missed; a sensor offline, not yet installed or retired records nothing, and the tables do not say when a sensor was offline. The network grew from 9 sensors in September 2021 to 68 in September 2023, so a count of sensors with events is mostly a count of where sensors were.

Facts of FloodNet: Sensor Deployment Metadata. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

Groundsource: A Dataset of Flood Events from News Google Research (Zenodo record, Rotem Mayo, Oleg Zlydenko et al.); facts confirmed; values to be recomputed by a second reader in F2; 19 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 9 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FS2, FS5, FV5, FT5. Known biases: Events exist only where news was digitised, published online and still reachable, in the 80 languages read; the paper states a recency bias (about 64% of events in 2020-2025, 15% in 2025) and uneven coverage by region. A row is a place named in an article, not a measured flood: one flood gives many rows, and a day with no row means no report was found, not no flood.

Table 2: "uuid  A unique identifier for each record. | area_km2  Area of the reported location polygon. | start_date  The initial day (formatted as YYYY-MM-DD) for which there is documented textual evidence of an ongoing flood. | end_date  The final consecutive day (formatted as YYYY-MM-DD) for which there is documented evidence of the flood. If the aggregated news reports only indicate flooding on a single day, the end date is identical to the start date. | geometry  The spatial boundary of the reported location, utilizing the standard WGS 84 coordinate reference system (EPSG:4326)..." ; text: "The dataset consists of 2,646,302 individual records." ; file: pyarrow num_rows=2646302, columns uuid:string, area_km2:double, geometry:binary, start_date:string, end_date:string, __index_level_0__:int64

The record page itself carries no schema documentation (its description is one sentence); the schema is documented in the preprint, found through the Google Research blog (https://research.google/blog/introducing-groundsource-turning-news-reports-into-data-with-gemini/).

geometry_representation furtherconfirmeddate range, place polygon, area_km2Polygon (2,478,877 rows) or MultiPolygon (167,425 rows), stored as WKB bytes in the column geometry, CRS WGS 84 EPSG:4326 (x = longitude, y = latitude), GeoParquet metadata version 0.4.0 written by geopandas. Not points: a place with no polygon in the geocoder is a point buffered by 0.001 degrees, which is a polygon.https://eartharxiv.org/repository/view/12083/ (Sections 2.3, Table 2); file geo metadata
passage
geo metadata: "encoding": "WKB", "crs": {... "name": "WGS 84" ... "id": {"authority": "EPSG", "code": 4326}}, "geometry_type": ["MultiPolygon", "Polygon"], "version": "0.4.0" ; paper 2.3: "a small spatial buffer of 0.001° is applied around the returned point to generate a representative, two-dimensional geometry" ; duckdb st_geometrytype: POLYGON 2478877, MULTIPOLYGON 167425
DEFECT in the file: the GeoParquet metadata uses the key "geometry_type" where the specification has "geometry_types"; DuckDB (2.0.1 with spatial) refuses to open the file with "Geoparquet column 'geometry' does not have geometry types". A reader must drop the geo metadata (done here with pyarrow replace_schema_metadata(None)) or read with pyarrow/geopandas. The CRS axis order in the PROJJSON is lat, lon, but the coordinates in the WKB are x=lon, y=lat (first row: lon 8.6, lat 54.8, which is in northern Europe).
area_km2_meaning furtherconfirmedDocument that area_km2 is the area of the named place, not of the flood.area_km2 is the area of the geometry polygon of the reported place: the documentation says "Area of the reported location polygon". Checked on the file: on a random sample of 30,000 rows, area_km2 equals the geodesic (spheroid) area of the row geometry within 1% in 99.9% of rows (median ratio 1.0000000001). So it is the place, not the flood.https://eartharxiv.org/repository/view/12083/ (Table 2)
passage
Table 2: "area_km2  Area of the reported location polygon." ; script f0/scripts/gs_checks.py: area_km2 / (ST_Area_Spheroid(geometry)/1e6) over 30000 sampled rows: quantiles [0, 1%, 5%, 50%, 95%, 99%, 100%] = 0.923, 0.9977, 0.9995, 1.0000, 1.00046, 1.0021, 1.067 ; share within 1% = 0.99913
The paper also says: "the dataset structure is entity-based rather than meteorology-based. Therefore, a single large-scale meteorological flood may result in multiple separate entries". Place size filter: events with area over 5,000 km2 or diameter over 500 km are discarded (file max area_km2 = 4998.83).
production furtherconfirmedhow the records were producedNews articles (about 7.5 million processed, about 5 million judged by the LLM to report an actual flood) read in 80 languages, translated to English with Cloud Translation, then Gemini extracts flooded places and dates; places are tied to WebRef entities or geocoded with the Google Maps Geocoding API to polygons; same-place reports on the same or consecutive days are merged into one row; places over 5,000 km2 or 500 km across, runs over 7 days and dates before 2000 are dropped.https://eartharxiv.org/repository/view/12083/ (Section 2) and https://research.google/blog/introducing-groundsource-turning-news-reports-into-data-with-gemini/
passage
paper: "In total, approximately 5 million of the 7.5 million processed articles were identified by the LLM as reporting an actual flood event." ; "Entries reporting the exact same location on the same day, or on consecutive days, are concatenated." ; blog: "The system identifies granular locations (neighborhoods and streets) and maps them to standardized spatial polygons using Google Maps Platform."
The paper is "a non-peer-reviewed preprint" and "an unreviewed preprint" (EarthArXiv, March 9, 2026).
precision furtherconfirmedstated precision or error ratesManual review of 400 random rows: 60% +/- 5% (95% CI) Accurate (place polygon and dates both match the article); a further 22% Approximate or Partial, so 82% practically useful; 18% Wrong. Earlier pipeline steps on 125 hand-labelled articles: classification 75% precision, 90% recall; dates 61% precision, 74% recall; places 64% precision, 69% recall. Recall against GDACS floods 2020-2026: 85% to 100% of severe events. Errors are stated not to be i.i.d.; no regional breakdown is given.https://eartharxiv.org/repository/view/12083/ (Section 4.1)
passage
"Based on this manual review, the pipeline achieved a precision of 60% ± 5% (95% CI), representing entries where both the extracted location and dates perfectly matched the source text (the 'Accurate' category). An additional 22% of the entries were classified as either 'Approximate' or 'Partial.' ... The remaining 18% of evaluated entries were classified as 'Wrong' and considered unusable." ; "we report only the global precision metric."
Failure modes named: same place name in several countries or states, date offsets from relative dates ("the past weekend", "last September" imputed as September 1st). The last matters here: 2021-09-01 is the first of a month.

Interruption Cost Estimate (ICE) Calculator 2.x Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory and Resource Innovations, Inc. (icecalculator.com); facts checked: 7 confirmed, 2 differ; 7 confirmed, 2 differs, 0 not checked, 2 not applicable

2 fact(s) differ from docs/flood/requirements.md: identifier, reuse_terms. Under FS6 the stage stops until the requirements are corrected.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FM5. Known biases: Costs are for interruptions up to 24 hours only (long outages are outside the survey-based method); the model is a survey-based econometric model (4,156 residential and 5,287 non-residential responses in 2.2); the underlying equations and coefficients are copyrighted and licensed, not published. The 2.0-versus-2.2 memorandum shows estimates changed between versions.

duration_limit furtherconfirmed24 hours or lessConfirmed by the FAQ for ICE 2: 'The focus of the updated ICE Calculator is on the costs of power interruptions lasting up to 24 hours.' reuse_terms furtherdifferswhether the damage-function coefficients may be used offline [V]NOT freely reusable offline. The online tool and its API stay in the public domain; the spreadsheets (the equations and coefficients) are available only under a licensing agreement; LBNL and partners hold copyright on the underlying models. The Phase 1 final report describes the econometric structure (residential: a GLM; non-residential: a Probit plus a GLM) and lists explanatory variables with significance, but no coefficient values were found in its text (grep of 'coefficient' hits only method sections; report not read in full). So a published coefficient set for offline use was not found; calling the API is the permitted route.https://ice-calc-docs.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/documents/FAQs+for+ICE+Calculator+2.pdf
passage
Those equations include both the explanatory variables included in the models and their coefficients. ... Berkeley Lab-and our partners-filed for and received copyright protection from the U.S. government. Users that wish to access the spreadsheets will now need to go through a process to secure a license. As always, access to the online version of the ICE Calculator-and the Application Programming Interface-will remain in the public domain.  ; site: 'Berkeley Lab and our research partners hold a copyright for the underlying models (e.g., the equations) used by the ICE Calculator.'

Post-tropical cyclone Ida (2021) flood map for New York City’s Jamaica Bay watershed Stevens Institute of Technology and co-authors (Kasaei, Orton, Ralston, Warner), via Mendeley Data; facts confirmed; 13 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 0 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FV3, FH3. Known biases: Jamaica Bay watershed only. A model product (about 50 m grid, no buildings, no explicit sewers, one lumped drain rate), not an observation, calibrated and scored on a handful of high-water marks, so its 0.20 m is an in-sample figure. The 8 or 18 marks behind it are not independent of the USGS set used elsewhere in this study.

rmse_0_20_m furtherconfirmedthe 0.20 m of the published research model [S] (Kasaei et al. 2025, HESS 29:2043, abstract)The abstract says RMSE of 20 cm against high-water marks: same number as 0.20 m. Caveat: the paper validates against seven USGS marks inside its modeled area plus one from the Community Flood Watch Project (eight for Ida); the Mendeley record says "18 locations" for the calibration using two storms (Ida and 29 Sep 2023). The uncalibrated base run (no drain rate) had RMSE 40 cm and mean error 13 cm. Calibrated drain rate 13 mm/h.https://hess.copernicus.org/articles/29/2043/2025/
passage
abstract: "We generate a spatially continuous flood map of Ida with a root-mean-square error (RMSE) of 20 cm when compared to high-water marks" ; text: "we reference surveyed high-water marks (HWMs) from the USGS website for the event, finding seven locations within our modeled area; one more HWM is from the Community Flood Watch Project" ; "The calibrated drain rate of 13 mm h −1 from our model" ; Mendeley: "based on a calibration process that utilized two storms and high watermarks at 18 locations."
The 20 cm is in-sample (a calibration error, not an independent test) on very few marks; do not compare a model validated against the full 159 marks to it as if like for like.

Lidar terrain for New York City: USGS 3DEP 1 m DEM and NYC 2017 topobathymetric lidar DEM U.S. Geological Survey (3DEP); City of New York Office of Technology and Innovation (OTI); facts checked: 10 confirmed, 2 differ, 3 not checked; 10 confirmed, 2 differs, 3 not checked, 1 not applicable

2 fact(s) differ from docs/flood/requirements.md: identifier, nyc_topobathy_2017_resolution_crs_datum. Under FS6 the stage stops until the requirements are corrected.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FD2. Known biases: The 1 m grid is a patchwork of lidar projects from 2013 to 2025, none from the Ida year; the city product is 1 ft and from 2017, so buildings, grading and drainage changes since then are absent. Bare-earth models remove buildings; hydro-flattening and hydro-enforcement alter water bodies and culverts, which matters for pluvial flooding.

OpenFEMA NFIP Redacted Claims (v3, entity NfipClaims) FEMA, Federal Insurance and Mitigation Administration (OpenFEMA); facts checked: 7 confirmed, 1 not checked; 7 confirmed, 0 differs, 1 not checked, 5 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FT5. Known biases: Only claims on NFIP policies: uninsured, under-insured or out-of-programme losses are missing, and a claim exists only if the owner held a policy and filed. Locations are coarse (0.1 degree, block group) and the dataset is explicitly not an official federal report. Policies cluster in mapped floodplains, so pluvial losses outside them are under-counted.

Facts of OpenFEMA NFIP Redacted Claims (v3, entity NfipClaims). Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FH6. Known biases: Point estimates only, not areal (the volume says they are not directly applicable to larger areas and an areal reduction factor is needed); estimates assume a stationary climate (historical data); the confidence bounds are 90% intervals and are not symmetric. NOAA Storm Events Database, bulk CSV files NOAA National Centers for Environmental Information (NCEI), from National Weather Service reports; facts checked: 10 confirmed, 1 differ, 1 not checked; 10 confirmed, 1 differs, 1 not checked, 5 not applicable

1 fact(s) differ from docs/flood/requirements.md: fields. Under FS6 the stage stops until the requirements are corrected.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FV5, FT5. Known biases: Events are what the National Weather Service office for a county chose to enter, from spotters, public, media, officials and gauges; reporting depends on population, observers and office practice, so a flash flood entry means somebody reported it, and no entry means only that nobody did. Damage fields are estimates and are often 0.00K.

listing: StormEvents_details-ftp_v1.0_d2021_c20260323.csv.gz, ..._d2021_c20261118.csv.gz, ..._d2021_c20261119.csv.gz ; same for d2023 ; StormEvents_locations-ftp_v1.0_d2021_c20260707.csv.gz, ..._c20261118, ..._c20261119 ; row counts from the fetched files via duckdb: YEAR 2021 61389, YEAR 2023 75593
A study must pin the creation date it used (c20261119 here). Earlier years are reprocessed from time to time, so counts can change between creation dates.
fieldsdiffersfields with unitsDetails file has 51 columns (BEGIN_YEARMONTH ... EVENT_NARRATIVE, DATA_SOURCE); the PDF documents 50 of them and does not describe DATA_SOURCE (value "CSV" in all 136,982 rows of the two files). Units: injuries and deaths in persons; DAMAGE_PROPERTY and DAMAGE_CROPS in dollars as text with K or M suffix ("10.00K = $10,000; 10.00M = $10,000,000"); MAGNITUDE in knots (wind) or inches (hail), not used for floods; BEGIN_RANGE and END_RANGE in miles; TOR_LENGTH miles; TOR_WIDTH yards; coordinates decimal degrees; FLOOD_CAUSE text ("Reported or estimated cause of the flood", e.g. Heavy Rain). Locations file: YEARMONTH, EPISODE_ID, EVENT_ID, LOCATION_INDEX, RANGE, AZIMUTH, LOCATION, LATITUDE, LONGITUDE, LAT2, LON2.https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/Storm-Data-Bulk-csv-Format.pdf
passage
PDF: "damage_property Ex: 10.00K, 0.00K, 10.00M The estimated amount of damage to property incurred by the weather event (e.g. 10.00K = $10,000; 10.00M = $10,000,000)" ; "begin_range Ex: 0.59, 0.69, 4.84, 1.17 (in miles)" ; "flood_cause Ex: Ice Jam, Heavy Rain, Heavy Rain/Snow Melt  Reported or estimated cause of the flood." ; locations PDF names "lat" and "lon", the 2021 locations file header is: YEARMONTH,EPISODE_ID,EVENT_ID,LOCATION_INDEX,RANGE,AZIMUTH,LOCATION,LATITUDE,LONGITUDE,LAT2,LON2
Outcome "differs" is about the documentation, not about requirements.md: the documentation (PDF) and the files disagree in three places. (1) Locations columns are LATITUDE, LONGITUDE, LAT2, LON2 plus YEARMONTH, not lat and lon; LAT2/LON2 look like the same coordinates times 100000 (3817400, 9859400 for 38.29/-98.99, not explained in the PDF). (2) Date-time text in the files is "01-SEP-21 22:00:00" (DD-MON-YY), the PDF shows MM/DD/YYYY. (3) DATA_SOURCE is undocumented. Here it is the publisher's documentation that differs from the publisher's files; the requirements make no claim about the fields.
flood_event_types furtherconfirmedFlash Flood, Flood, Heavy Rain, Coastal FloodAll four exist in the permitted event list. Flash Flood and Flood and Heavy Rain are county-based (C); Coastal Flood is zone-based (Z). Also in the list: Lakeshore Flood (Z), Storm Surge/Tide (Z), Debris Flow (C).https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/Storm-Data-Bulk-csv-Format.pdf
passage
PDF Table from NWS Directive 10-1605 section 2.1.1: "Coastal Flood Z ... Debris Flow C ... Flash Flood C ... Flood C ... Heavy Rain C ... Lakeshore Flood Z ... Storm Surge/Tide Z"
Consequence: a Coastal Flood entry for New York City is written against a forecast zone (e.g. SOUTHERN QUEENS), not a county, so a county filter misses it (the two files hold one: SOUTHERN QUEENS, zone type Z).
place_representation furtherconfirmedcounty or zone FIPS, begin and end coordinatesEach event carries CZ_TYPE (C county or parish, Z NWS public forecast zone, M marine), CZ_FIPS (county FIPS or zone number), CZ_NAME, STATE and STATE_FIPS, WFO, plus BEGIN_LAT/BEGIN_LON and END_LAT/END_LON (decimal degrees; in the New York rows 2 to 4 decimals) and begin/end range-azimuth-location text. Further points in the locations file (LOCATION_INDEX per event). For the five New York City counties: CZ_TYPE C, CZ_FIPS (unpadded in the file) 5 Bronx, 47 Kings, 61 New York, 81 Queens, 85 Richmond.https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/Storm-Data-Bulk-csv-Format.pdf

time_zone furtherconfirmedtime zone handlingTimes are in local standard time of the county or zone ("usually in LST"), with the zone in CZ_TIMEZONE. All 122 rows for the five New York City counties in 2021 and 2023 say EST-5, including events in September when New York is on daylight time, so the text clock is EST (UTC-5), one hour behind the wall clock. Dates here were matched on that EST clock as written.https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/Storm-Data-Bulk-csv-Format.pdf
passage
PDF: "begin_date_time Ex: 04/1/2012 20:48:00 MM/DD/YYYY hh:mm:ss (24 hour time usually in LST)" ; "cz_timezone Ex: EST-5, MST-7, CST-6 Time Zone for the County/Parish, Zone or Marine Name." ; file: select CZ_TIMEZONE,count(*) from n group by 1 -> [(EST-5,122)]
Sensitivity of the counts to the clock: shifting all times +1 hour (EST to EDT) changes events covering 2021-09-01 from 36 to 35, 2021-09-02 from 4 to 8, and 2023-09-29 stays 15. "usually" means the PDF does not guarantee LST for every row.

311 Service Requests from 2020 to Present NYC Office of Technology and Innovation (OTI), on NYC Open Data; facts confirmed; 11 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 1 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FV5, FT5. Known biases: Each row is a call, web or app report made by a person, so counts follow who knows the service and reports, where people are at home or online, and duplicate reports of one event (the dataset has no flag for duplicates). It holds only 'service requests that can be directed to specific agencies'. A report is not a measured flood and says nothing about depth; absence of complaints in an area is not absence of flooding.

Facts of 311 Service Requests from 2020 to Present. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

complaint_type | text | This is the first level of a hierarchy identifying the topic of the incident or condition.
descriptor | text | This is associated to the Problem (formerly Complaint Type), and provides further detail on the incident or condition.
This dataset is updated daily and expected values for many fields will change over time. The lists of expected values associated with each column are not exhaustive.
flood_complaint_types furtherconfirmed–Two naming schemes for the same DEP sewer requests appear in 2021 to 2023 data. Legacy: complaint_type 'Sewer' with descriptors 'Street Flooding (SJ)', 'Highway Flooding (SH)', 'Sewer Backup (Use Comments) (SA)', 'Catch Basin Clogged/Flooding (Use Comments) (SC)', 'Catch Basin Sunken/Damaged/Raised (SC1)', 'Catch Basin Grating Missing (SA4)', 'Catch Basin Search (SC2)', 'Manhole Overflow (Use Comments) (SA1)', 'Culvert Blocked/Needs Cleaning (SE)'. Current: complaint_type 'Sewer Maintenance' with descriptors 'Flooding on Street', 'Flooding on Highway', 'Backup', 'Catch Basin Clogged', 'Catch Basin Sunken', 'Catch Basin Grate Missing', 'Manhole Overflow', 'Culvert'. Both appear on 2023-09-29 (legacy 'Sewer/Sewer Backup' 923, current 'Sewer Maintenance/Backup' 870 in Brooklyn and Queens); on 2021-09-01/02 no 'Sewer Maintenance' rows appear in Brooklyn and Queens, only the legacy names. Related: 'Water System / Excessive Water In Basement (WEFB)' (2021) and 'Water Maintenance / Water in Basement' (2023); 'Standing Water' (a handful); 'Water Drainage / Drain/Pipe Clogged'.https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/erm2-nwe9.json?$select=complaint_type,descriptor,count(*)&$group=complaint_type,descriptor

Query form: https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/erm2-nwe9.json?$select=borough,count(*)&$group=borough&$where=created_date >= '<day>T00:00:00' AND created_date < '<next day>T00:00:00' AND borough in('BROOKLYN','QUEENS') AND (<condition>) . Conditions: street flooding = (complaint_type='Sewer' AND descriptor='Street Flooding (SJ)') OR (complaint_type='Sewer Maintenance' AND descriptor='Flooding on Street'); sewer backup = (complaint_type='Sewer' AND descriptor='Sewer Backup (Use Comments) (SA)') OR (complaint_type='Sewer Maintenance' AND descriptor='Backup'); catch basin = (complaint_type='Sewer' AND descriptor='Catch Basin Clogged/Flooding (Use Comments) (SC)') OR (complaint_type='Sewer Maintenance' AND descriptor='Catch Basin Clogged'). created_date taken as given (no time-zone conversion; the API does not state one). Highway flooding (SH) and other catch basin descriptors are excluded.

DoITT may require third party entities such as application developers to explicitly identify the source, version, and modifications made to a public data set, should they publicly re-publish it elsewhere or incorporate the public data set into an application. ... By accessing data sets and feeds available through the NYC OpenData portal (or the “Site”), the user agrees to all of the Terms of Use of NYC.gov as well as the NYC.gov Privacy Policy. The user also agrees to any additional terms of use defined by entities providing data or feeds through the Site. ... Public data sets made available on the NYC OpenData portal are provided for informational purposes. The City does not warranty the completeness, accuracy, content, or fitness for any particular purpose or use of any public data set made available on the NYC OpenData portal ...

Building Elevation and Subgrade (BES) NYC Department of City Planning (DCP), on NYC Open Data; facts confirmed; 16 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 1 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FD5. Known biases: Everything comes from what a vendor's operators could see in street-level photos and LiDAR from the road: buildings hidden by construction or obstruction (135,503 rows) have estimated grade, 14,039 rows have no measurement (value 0), and subgrade space reachable only from inside (cellars in large buildings) is not recorded, so 'no subgrade' means 'none seen'. Small accessory buildings such as sheds are excluded. The 1,000-row sample fetched is the first 1,000 by BIN and covers Manhattan only.

Facts of Building Elevation and Subgrade (BES). Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

Each record contains a grade and first floor measurement for each building (recorded as feet above sea-level in the NADV88 vertical datum) and indicates if subgrade space exists.
The description misspells the datum as "NADV88" in two places; the column descriptions and dictionary say NAVD88.
grade_estimates furtherconfirmedread the guidance note on estimated gradesGrade and floor were read by vendor operators from street-level LiDAR point clouds and photo imagery (captured every 16 feet, March to July 2021 per the dictionary). Where the lowest adjacent grade was below street level or hidden, the value is an estimate from context clues (door or window height); the estimate is flagged only through notes1 ('Property not Visible Due to Construction or Obstruction', 135,503 rows, or 'Property not Visible from Street', 12,082 rows; 147,585 in all), and there is no record of which of grade or floor was estimated. A value of 0 means no measurement: z_grade = 0 in 14,039 rows, z_floor = 0 in 14,038 (14,038 rows have both at 0, one row has z_grade 0 only). The estimated accuracy is called 'reasonable' without a figure.https://data.cityofnewyork.us/api/views/bsin-59hv/files/ae1b369c-6ce1-4805-9ad0-aca9f62f3182
passage
In many instances where the lowest adjacent grade was below street level, the derived measurement in the dataset is an estimate. Any building centroid that has an estimated grade elevation will note “Property not visible due to construction or obstruction” under note_1. Estimates were made by individual data operators based on context clues in the imagery.
[dictionary] While note_1 records if estimates were taken, there is no record of which of the two observed measurements (or both) was the estimated value. DCP has established that the accuracy of estimated spot elevations is reasonable provided that one of the methodologies previously described has been followed.
[dictionary, z_grade] When no measurement has been taken, the field value will be 0
The guidance PDF and the Excel dictionary disagree on dates: the dataset description says imagery "collected ... between March and July of 2021" and the limitations row says "only records what was captured by the data vendor between March and August of 2022"; the footprints are the DOB file of 2022-02-26. Counts from API queries: notes1 like '%Construction or Obstruction%' = 135503; notes1 like '%Not Visible%' or '%not Visible%' = 147585; z_grade=0 -> 14039; z_grade=0 and z_floor=0 -> 14038; z_floor=0 and z_grade>0 -> 0.

NYC Building Footprints (BUILDING, `5zhs-2jue`) NYC Office of Technology and Innovation (OTI, formerly DoITT), on NYC Open Data; facts checked: 7 confirmed, 1 differ; 7 confirmed, 1 differs, 0 not checked, 1 not applicable

1 fact(s) differ from docs/flood/requirements.md: identifier. Under FS6 the stage stops until the requirements are corrected.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F1. Used for FD5. Known biases: Footprints are updated daily by city staff, so the layer is the city as of the download, not as of a storm date; construction_year is reliable only after 2017 and mostly from the tax roll before 2002 (accurate to a decade at best). ground_elevation is a lowest-adjacent-ground value in feet, NAVD88 only for photogrammetric or modern sources, with accuracy that varies; height_roof is zero or null where unavailable. The BIN is not unique (million BINs for unassigned buildings) and a building may be split in several footprints sharing a BIN. Garages (feature code 5110) and other auxiliary structures are in the layer. Numbers in the GeoJSON are strings.

Facts of NYC Building Footprints (BUILDING, `5zhs-2jue`). Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

fieldsconfirmedBIN, BBL, construction year, roof height `heightroof`, ground elevation `groundelev`, feature code, last statusThe Socrata table has 16 columns: the_geom, name, bin, doitt_id, shape_area, base_bbl, objectid, construction_year, feature_code, geom_source, ground_elevation, height_roof, last_edited_date, last_status_type, mappluto_bbl, shape_length. The names `heightroof` and `groundelev` are the SHAPEFILE names (HEIGHTROOF, GROUNDELEV); in the Socrata table and the GeoJSON they are `height_roof` and `ground_elevation`. Units: ground_elevation is feet, lowest elevation at the building ground level, NAVD88 when collected photogrammetrically or from modern sources, accuracy varies; height_roof is feet above ground elevation (not above sea level), zero or null means not available. construction_year: year completed, accurate for buildings built after 2017; before 2002 mostly from the tax roll, accurate to a decade at best; null (or 0) means not available. feature_code: 1000 Parking, 1001 Gas Station Canopy, 1002 Storage Tank, 1003 Placeholder (triangle for permitted bldg), 1004 Auxiliary Structure, 1005 Temporary Structure, 1006 Cantilevered Building, 2100 Building, 2110 Skybridge, 5100 Building Under Construction, 5110 Garage. last_status_type: Demolition, Alteration, Geometry, Initialization, Correction, Marked for Construction, Marked For Demolition, Constructed (also seen in the box: Split, Merged, Investigate Construction). bin: DOB building id, first digit borough (1 Manhattan, 2 Bronx, 3 Brooklyn, 4 Queens, 5 Staten Island); BINs ending 000000 (1000000, 3000000 ...) mean unassigned or unknown, so the BIN is not unique. base_bbl: tax lot the footprint lies in (text); mappluto_bbl: the key to join MapPLUTO (condominium billing BBL). doitt_id is the consistent unique building key; objectid is a synthetic key ("Use DOITT_ID instead"). shape_area and shape_length: "Do not use" (Web Mercator, not area preserving). Horizontal: published in WGS84 (GeoJSON), captured in NY State Plane Long Island East, NAD83, US foot (EPSG:2263). In the GeoJSON every property value is a string, numbers included.https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CityOfNewYork/nyc-geo-metadata/master/Metadata/Metadata_BuildingFootprints.md
passage
GROUND_ELEVATION | GROUNDELEV | Lowest elevation at the building ground level, in feet. Calculated from LiDAR or photogrammetrically. | number | Accuracy varies but when collected photogrammetrically or from modern sources this value is based on the North American Vertical Datum of 1988 ; HEIGHT_ROOF | HEIGHTROOF | Roof height above ground elevation, in feet (not above sea level). | number | Records where this is zero or NULL mean that this information was not available. ; BIN ... In some cases where these 6 digits are all zeros (e.g. 1000000, 2000000, etc.) the BIN is unassigned or unknown. ... Due to "million BINs" this identifier is not unique ; FEATURE_CODE | FEAT_CODE | Type of Building. List of values: 1000 = Parking 1001 = Gas Station Canopy ... 5110 = Garage
Features captured: all buildings over 400 sq ft and taller than 12 ft, plus others with BINs; excluded: interior divisions, temporary trailers or tents, jet bridges, awnings, scaffolds, sidewalk sheds, small backyard sheds with no visible car access. Positional accuracy for photogrammetric features +/- 2 ft (95%); manual features less accurate. The ground_elevation datum is stated conditionally (NAVD88 only when collected photogrammetrically or from modern sources): older values may not be NAVD88. The portal's column descriptions for construction_year, feature_code, ground_elevation, height_roof, last_status_type are empty; the meanings come from the GitHub metadata page.

NYC Stormwater Flood Maps NYC Department of Environmental Protection (DEP), published on NYC Open Data; facts checked: 11 confirmed, 2 differ, 2 not checked; values to be recomputed by a second reader in F2; 11 confirmed, 2 differs, 2 not checked, 1 not applicable

2 fact(s) differ from docs/flood/requirements.md: min_area_and_depth_rule, depth_classes. Under FS6 the stage stops until the requirements are corrected.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FS1, FH3. Known biases: Rain-only maps from a model with the drainage system assumed fully working and catch basins clear; parks of 100,000 sq ft or more, non-residential non-commercial lots of 250,000 sq ft or more and lots with railway were masked out, so flooding there is not shown. Areas under 0.25 acre are dropped (with exceptions), and coastal storm surge is not modelled. The publisher says very little observed data on surface flooding exists to check the maps against.

The plan describes only two scenarios (Moderate, Extreme). The two current-sea-level layers and the 2.13/1.77/3.66 figures are not in the plan: it says "approximately two inches of rain falling in one hour" and "approximately 3.5 inches of rain falling in one hour". The plan is dated May 2021, the maps were republished in 2024; whether the plan describes the 2024 maps exactly is not stated.
min_area_and_depth_rule furtherdiffersthey show areas of at least 0.25 contiguous acres with 4 inches or more **[S]**The rule is stated as claimed, but the data dictionary adds an exception: the dataset 'contains some potential flooding areas less than 0.25 acres due to proximity and overlap with masking layers'. The plan says the threshold was set 'through an iterative approach' and uses 'greater-than-or-equal-to four (4) inches' in one place and 'greater than 4 inches' in another.Inside the zip: each layer dictionary, sheet Dataset Information, row "How is this data collected?"; plan Appendix B, Mapping approach
passage
The New York City Stormwater Flood Maps show areas of potential flooding of at least 0.25 contiguous acres with flood depths of 4 inches or more.  This dataset contains some potential flooding areas less than 0.25 acres due to proximity and overlap with masking layers.
Outcome is "differs" only in the sense that the rule is not strict; the rule itself is confirmed. A loader must not assume every polygon part is at least 0.25 acre.
depth_classes furtherdiffersthe two depth classes (4 in to 1 ft, over 1 ft)Two depth classes exist (code 1 = 4 in to under 1 ft, code 2 = 1 ft and greater), but the two layers with sea-level rise carry a THIRD code, 3, which is not a depth class: 'Future High Tides 2050' / '2080', coastal tidal inundation taken from the NYC Flood Hazard Mapper. The two current-sea-level layers have codes 1 and 2 only (per their dictionaries; the Limited layer could not be read, see 'layers').Inside the zip: Column Information sheet of each layer dictionary, row Flooding_Category
passage
[2080 and 2050 layers] 1 - Nuisance Flooding (greater or equal to 4 in. and less than 1 ft.)
2 - Deep and Contiguous Flooding (1 ft. and greater)
3 - Future High Tides 2080   (2050 layer: Future High Tides 2050)
[Current-sea-level layers] 1 - Nuisance Flooding (greater or equal to 4 in. and less than 1 ft.)
2 - Deep and Contiguous Flooding (1 ft. and greater)
[Plan] The maps depict two ranges of ponding depth: 1) Areas with ponding depth greater than 4 inches 2) Areas with ponding depth greater than 1 foot. In the maps, the areas with ponding depth greater than 1 foot are displayed on top of the areas greater than 4 inches
Plan: the 1-foot areas are drawn on top of the 4-inch areas; in the geodatabases the three classes are separate non-overlapping-by-code rows (one multipolygon per code), I did not test whether code 1 geometry also contains code 2 areas. The plan says tidal layers are "overlaid in the map on top of all rainfall-derived flooding". The loader should drop or separate code 3 before rasterising depth classes.

The plan (2021) describes its Moderate scenario as "approximately two inches of rain falling in one hour" (10-year) and Extreme as "approximately 3.5 inches of rain falling in one hour" (100-year), i.e. one hour, but with rounded figures, not 2.13 and 3.66, and it has no Limited scenario. This scenario reflects approximately two inches of rain falling in one hour (also referred to as the 10-year storm, with approximately 10 percent chance of occurrence in any given year) combined with 2.5 feet of SLR ... This scenario reflects approximately 3.5 inches of rain falling in one hour (also referred to as the 100-year storm, with approximately 1 percent chance of occurrence in any given year) combined with 4.8 feet of SLR
Duration of the 1.77, 2.13 and 3.66 in/hr rainfalls: not found in a primary source; do not state one. The "in/hr" label together with the plan suggests one-hour design storms but that is an inference. The plan also says 1.75 in/hr is the 5-year one-hour storm, close to but not equal to 1.77; the plan does not say the Limited map uses it. Return periods against Atlas 14 are for F3.
model furtherconfirmedmodel used **[V]**Hydrologic and hydraulic (H&H) models built in Innovyze InfoWorks ICM: 13 models, one per sewershed draining to a water resource recovery facility, 1D sewer network coupled to a 2D overland mesh (Phase 2), from the City calibrated LTCP models. 10-year scenarios: direct 1D-2D run. 100-year scenario: a composite approach (5-year run, then the remaining 100-year minus 5-year volume placed on the 2D mesh without sewers). Oakwood Beach used rain-on-mesh only. Plan is dated May 2021; the dictionaries only say "hydrologic and hydraulic computer models".https://www1.nyc.gov/assets/orr/pdf/publications/stormwater-resiliency-plan.pdf
passage
The models were built in Innovyze’s InfoWorks ICM software which facilitates coupling of a 1-dimensionsal (1-D) rainfall-runoff and sewer routing component with a 2-dimensional (2D) overland flow and flood visualization component.
Phase 2 modeling developed an H&H model for each hydraulically independent area of the city, resulting in 13 different models
The plan describes the Moderate (10-yr) and Extreme (100-yr) maps. Which approach produced the 1.77 in/hr Limited map is not stated. Whether a given layer used the 1D-2D or the composite approach is stated only for 10-yr and 100-yr.

tiles_for_box furtherconfirmedwhether a part covering only this box can be fetchedThe margin box is x 985,876 to 1,002,471 ft, y 187,747 to 204,341 ft in EPSG:2263. It touches four tiles: HEUF_NYC_020 (819,588,568 B), HEUF_NYC_021 (1,037,100,899 B), HEUF_NYC_025 (673,920,434 B), HEUF_NYC_026 (998,950,107 B), 3,529,560,008 B in all, so whole tiles exceed the 300 MB limit and nothing was downloaded. A part can be fetched without the whole tile: a 512 x 512 window of HEUF_NYC_021 was read over /vsicurl/ftp (range reads of the internal tiles; values 12.78 to 26.0 ft, mean 17.96) in a few seconds. NOAA Analysis of Record for Calibration (AORC) v1.1 NOAA Office of Water Prediction (OWP), served through the NOAA Open Data Dissemination (NODD) program on AWS; facts confirmed; 8 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 1 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FH5, FT5. Known biases: Registry notice of 2026-04-02: about 0.04% of rows were incorrectly masked by brittle pipeline components and the Zarr files were being regenerated; whether corrected data are posted was not found. Data are produced with a 10-day lag so that corrections to the input Stage IV and NLDAS data are included, so AORC precipitation is not independent of Stage IV. Version 1.0 had issues that v1.1 addressed (Fall et al. 2023, Table 1).

Public figures for the replacement or construction cost of electrical substations, by voltage class Several: MISO; Black & Veatch for WECC; CAISO participating transmission owners (PG&E, SCE); Consolidated Edison (NY PSC Case 22-E-0064); facts checked: 4 confirmed, 2 not checked; 4 confirmed, 0 differs, 2 not checked, 8 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F1. Used for FD4. Known biases: None of these is a replacement value of an existing urban substation. MISO and B&V price transmission substations on greenfield, flat, rural assumptions; the California guides price generator interconnection equipment; Con Edison's figures are forecasts for two large indoor New York City substations. Price years differ (2012 or earlier, 2023, 2024, 2025) and some are not stated; figures are reported as stated and are not adjusted. The latest MISO guide (MTEP26, June 2026) keeps its tables in a workbook that was not opened. No distribution (area) substation figure except Con Edison's Idlewild forecast was found.

So the newest substation figures, MTEP26 in 2026 dollars at +4% escalation from 2025, are NOT checked. No secondary summary is quoted here.
cost_miso_mtep26_guide_status furthernot checkedMISO Transmission Cost Estimation Guide, latest yearThe latest guide is the MTEP26 edition dated June 10, 2026 (PDF 939,975 bytes). Its text says all tables are in MISO's Exploratory Cost Estimate Workbook for MTEP26 and that its cost data are in 2026 U.S. dollars, escalated from 2025 at 4% for most cost categories. f2_curves furtherconfirmedcurves F2.1-F2.3 (same shape for the three substation size classes); damage begins at flood onset, 0 m; about 7% at 1.2 m and 15% at about 3 mF2.1 (Low Voltage), F2.2 (Medium Voltage), F2.3 (High Voltage) are identical at every depth. Defined from 0 to 3.05 m at 0.05 m steps (62 points); damage factor 0 at 0 m, 0.00328 at 0.05 m (damage begins at once), 0.0694 at 1.2 m, 0.1569 at 3.0 m, 0.16 at 3.05 m, the last point. karagiannakis_4_4_1 furtherconfirmedHazus anchor values (about 7% at 1.2 m, 15% at about 3 m), section 4.4.1Confirmed, from the arXiv copy 2504.20056 (pdf stamped as the Wiley OA article): section 4.4.1 Flood says HAZUS (FEMA 2013) classifies substations into low, medium and high voltage classes with high vulnerability; threshold of almost 1.2 m with 7% equipment damage for all classes; 15% at the maximum considered depth of approximately 3 m; equipment assumed about 0.91 m above ground. This is Karagiannakis's description of Hazus, not the Hazus table.https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.20056
passage
A threshold of almost 1.2 m, representing 7% equipment damage, is applied to all classes. The damage percentage increases to 15% at the maximum considered flooding depth of approximately 3 m (the depth is measured from ground level, assuming that equipment is located about 0.91 m above ground).
Secondary copy of the article (arXiv, arXiv API says arxiv:doi 10.1002/wcc.930).

National Structure Inventory (NSI) 2026 Base U.S. Army Corps of Engineers (NSI development team); facts checked: 11 confirmed, 1 not checked; 11 confirmed, 0 differs, 1 not checked, 1 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FD5. Known biases: A modeled inventory, not a survey: attributes are estimated from parcels, footprints, Census data and regional distributions, so foundation height, basement and values are typical rather than measured. Prices are August 2025, the inventory reflects roughly 2025 buildings and population, and stacked structures are counted as several records on one footprint.

Facts of National Structure Inventory (NSI) 2026 Base. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

fieldsconfirmedoccupancy type, foundation type or basement, first-floor height found_ht, replacement value val_struct, stories, ground elevationAll present in the public 2026 schema and in my sample: occtype (e.g. RES3C, COM4; Hazus-style damage function name), st_damcat (RES/COM/IND/PUB), found_type (C crawl, B basement, S slab, P pier, I pile, F fill, W solid wall), found_ht ("foundation height of the structure in feet from the ground elevation"), num_story, val_struct (depreciated replacement value in dollars), val_cont, val_vehic, fullrep (full non-depreciated replacement value, new in 2026), depindex, sqft, ftprntsqft, bldheight (metres), ground_elv (feet, NAVD88) and grnd_elv_m (metres, NAVD88), med_yr_blt (median of the census tract, not the building), firmzone, zone_sub, static_bfe, x/y WGS84. A basement is only the found_type=B flag: the detailed `basement` field (finished/unfinished/none) is a restricted field for federal users and mission partners. "What is the price level of the NSI? NSI dollar values represent estimated replacement costs as of approximately August 2025. NSI values reflect full and depreciated replacement cost, not market value. They exclude land value and broader market effects such as location premiums." ; technical documentation: "All values are reported in August 2025 price levels."
Event-year losses for 2021 would need deflating by a price index; the NSI itself gives none.

known_limits furtherconfirmed–Stated by the publisher: a modeled national inventory, not a structure-by-structure verification; errors possible in location, occupancy, square footage, stories, foundation type and height, population, value; meant for screening and regional use; "not intended for legal or regulatory determinations"; local review and surveys recommended; stacked structures (several records on one footprint, with value divided); structure placed at footprint or parcel centroid; garages not separate.https://www.hec.usace.army.mil/confluence/nsi/technicalreferences/latest/technical-documentation
passage
"The NSI Base dataset should be understood as a modeled national exposure inventory rather than a structure-by-structure verification dataset." ; "individual structure records may contain errors in location, occupancy type, square footage, number of stories, foundation type, foundation height, population, or value." ; FAQ: "NSI is not intended for legal or regulatory determinations or for analyses requiring precise structure-level accuracy."
In my sample 1,586 records sit on 1,586 fd_id but fewer footprints (stacked structures); count distinct ftprntid to count buildings (FAQ advice).

join_fields_to_city_buildings furtherconfirmedwhich identifier fields could join it to the city's buildingsThere is no BIN, BBL or DOITT id in the 42 fields. What exists: (1) x, y: WGS84 longitude and latitude of the structure point (all inside the box; the structure is placed at the footprint or parcel centroid), so the join to city footprints or BES centroids is spatial (point in polygon, or nearest); (2) fd_id: integer, unique per record (22,896 distinct), the NSI's own record key; (3) bid: text built on an Open Location Code (plus code) of the footprint plus suffixes, for example "87G8M2RX+Q8H-3-4-3-4", filled in 11,596 records, empty ("") in the others; (4) ftprntid: footprint id, either "none_<number>" (11,300 records) or "<county FIPS>_<number>" such as "36047_158616" (5,647 distinct values over the other 11,596 records, so several records share a footprint: stacked structures); (5) usastrucid: filled in 9,744 records (for example "7913338"), empty in the others; (6) cbfips: 15-digit Census 2020 block id; (7) ftprntsrc: footprint source (P 11,280; NGA 9,657; Bing 1,852; ORNL 87; H 20). None of the ids was matched to a city id here.https://nsi.sec.usace.army.mil/nsiapi/structures?bbox=-73.9900,40.6850,-73.9900,40.7245,-73.9385,40.7245,-73.9385,40.6850,-73.9900,40.6850

USGS 3DEP 1 m DEM tiles over the F1 study box (north Brooklyn, 500 m margin) U.S. Geological Survey, 3D Elevation Program; facts confirmed; 13 confirmed, 0 differs, 0 not checked, 2 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F1. Used for FD2, FH2. Known biases: Bare-earth model from 2013-14 lidar: buildings, grading, drainage and shoreline changes since then are absent. Water is hydro-flattened (a flat surface, here at -1.62 m), so water depth and channel shape are not terrain. Elevations are NAD83/NAVD88 metres; the tile CRS is EPSG:26918 (NAD83), the project CRS EPSG:32618 (WGS84); the two differ by about a metre at most and the numbers were used unchanged. Flight dates are a project range, not per-flight dates.

water_treatment furtherconfirmedwater is often hydro-flattened: what does the metadata sayThe tile metadata carries only the theme keyword Hydro-Flattened and no description of how water was treated. In the raster, 2,570,523 pixels (10.28 % of the margin box, 1,846,518 in the study square) hold exactly -1.62 m, one constant value, consistent with a flat water surface; 32,846 pixels are below -5 m and 28,615 below -10 m (minimum -13.49), values near -12.8 m being the most common among them. What those deep values are (channel or dredged-area bathymetry, or artefacts) is not stated in the metadata and was not determined.https://prd-tnm.s3.amazonaws.com/StagedProducts/Elevation/1m/Projects/NY_CMPG_2013/metadata/USGS_one_meter_x58y451_NY_CMPG_2013_meta.xml

<themekey>Hydro-Flattened</themekey>
The reading of -1.62 m as the flattened river surface is an inference from the constant value. Pixels at or below 0 m are water for the most part; they are not land terrain.

identifiernot applicable–A publication or a rule, not a data file: it has no such fact.–
Geospatial datasets of water surface elevation and water depth in New York City associated with the remnants of Hurricane Ida, September 1, 2021 U.S. Geological Survey, New York Water Science Center (via ScienceBase); facts checked: 13 confirmed, 1 not checked; 13 confirmed, 0 differs, 1 not checked, 0 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FV1. Known biases: Covers only discs of 250 m radius around 83 surveyed marks; everywhere else there is no depth. The depth is the mark minus lidar ground, assuming flat water, so it is derived from the marks and not an independent observation. Drains and subway spaces are ignored and the lidar is 2017, four years before the event.

Facts of Geospatial datasets of water surface elevation and water depth in New York City associated with the remnants of Hurricane Ida, September 1, 2021. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

what_the_depth_is furtherconfirmed–NOT a hydrodynamic model. Depth = surveyed HWM elevation minus lidar ground elevation, within a 250 m circle around each mark, assuming a single constant water surface across that circle, connected low ground only, ground more than 1 ft below the mark treated as flowing water and removed. Sinks (subways, storm drains) ignored. Buildings inundated only if z_floor <= water level and the building has a subgrade space.https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/file/get/63badf22d34e92aad3cd279e?f=__disk__74%2F82%2F91%2F74829135b81999cf615c9a3416efcb9003c0de8f
passage
"These depth maps assume a constant water-surface elevation across the landscape around each HWM and show where potential inundation may have occurred during the rainfall event." ; "1) The extent of inundation was limited by the land-surface elevation within a 250-meter distance buffer around each HWM location." ; "5) All potential sinks were ignored."
For validation this means the raster at a mark is almost a restatement of that mark (depth = mark minus lidar); it is not an independent observation away from the marks.
resolution furtherconfirmed–1-ft cells ("1-ft discretization"), snapped to the source lidar. Checked on one raster (NYBRO31256_InundationDepth.tif): extent 1032257..1033231 ft by 236491..236935 ft = 974 x 444 pixels, so 1 ft cells. Depth values in feet.https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/file/get/63badf22d34e92aad3cd279e?f=__disk__74%2F82%2F91%2F74829135b81999cf615c9a3416efcb9003c0de8f
passage
"The polygons were converted to a tiff file raster with a 1-ft discretization and snapped to match the source lidar." ; "The water depth data is presented as tiff files (raster format), in feet above land-surface elevation (Quantum Spatial, 2018)"
The metadata sentence "The lidar has a 1-ft discretization" is ambiguous (cell size or vertical rounding). Pixel size taken from the raster itself; vertical rounding of the lidar values not checked.

method furtherconfirmed–ArcMap 10.8.1 raster calculator: [HWM elevation raster] minus [lidar mosaicked with building z_floor from the DCP Building Elevation and Subgrade table]; one mark per site (best quality grade, higher elevation if tied). Validation: "ensure that the inundation depths presented make logical sense"; no numerical error statistic given. Accuracy depends on HWM, lidar, and first-floor data.https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/file/get/63badf22d34e92aad3cd279e?f=__disk__74%2F82%2F91%2F74829135b81999cf615c9a3416efcb9003c0de8f

USGS high-water marks for the remnants of Hurricane Ida, five boroughs of New York City U.S. Geological Survey, New York Water Science Center (via ScienceBase); facts checked: 11 confirmed, 1 differ, 2 not checked; values to be recomputed by a second reader in F5; 11 confirmed, 1 differs, 2 not checked, 0 not applicable

1 fact(s) differ from docs/flood/requirements.md: mark_count. Under FS6 the stage stops until the requirements are corrected.

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F0. Used for FV1. Known biases: Marks exist only where crews found and surveyed debris, mud or seed lines, up to 2.5 months after the event, in the five boroughs (159 marks at 83 sites; 54 sites in Brooklyn and Queens). Areas without such evidence, and basements and subway spaces, are not represented. Horizontal positions are map or phone fixes (WGS84 from digital map), and the whole set is tagged "Riverine" and mostly "swift water" although the event was pluvial.

Facts of USGS high-water marks for the remnants of Hurricane Ida, five boroughs of New York City. Each fact as read from the primary source on the date shown; claimed is what the requirements say. Facts are not a recomputation of values.

Aerial imagery of the F1 area: USGS NAIP (TNM image services) and NYS orthoimagery USGS / USDA Farm Service Agency (NAIP); New York State ITS GIS Program Office (NYS orthoimagery); facts checked: 10 confirmed, 2 not checked; 10 confirmed, 0 differs, 2 not checked, 3 not applicable

Checked on 2026-11-25 in F1. Used for FD3. Known biases: Imagery is for looking at substations, not for measurement; each image is a single date (NAIP 2019-08-30 in leaf-on summer, NYS 2018 in spring). It does not show the study events and cannot show change since its date.

naip_request furtherconfirmedhow a small picture is requestedexportImage on the ImageServer with bbox, bboxSR, imageSR, size, format and f=image. The request used (400 m square in EPSG:32618 so the ground size is exact; image 800 x 800 px = 0.5 m per pixel, finer than the 0.6 m native): https://imagery.nationalmap.gov/arcgis/rest/services/USGSNAIPImagery/ImageServer/exportImage?bbox=586727.18,4506208.19,587127.18,4506608.19&bboxSR=32618&imageSR=32618&size=800,800&format=png&f=image .
